The two crowded pillars are P2 and P4, where the literature is dense and
the project accordingly narrows its claim to measurement rather than to
a new algorithm. The widest open ground remains the systems and
reporting side, where no GRPO-specific machine-readable standard and no
infrastructure-level integrity benchmark exists --- the gap Section 2.4
establishes from three directions: HELM's unit is the (model, scenario)
cell rather than the training stack, the LM Evaluation Harness
standardises evaluation by executing code while the registry must
describe stacks it cannot execute, and Papers with Code stopped at the
repository boundary and was discontinued in July 2025 (sources:
\texttt{platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p6\_related\_resources.tex};
\texttt{platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex}).

The Semester 4 held-out campaign extends this positioning from
method-comparison to benchmark practice. A single Tinker-trained actor
--- base \texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B} with the
\texttt{pavlov-portfolio-qwen36-seed809-stepfinal} adapter, in bfloat16
--- was evaluated against fourteen held-out agentic and reasoning suites
under receipt, budget, and decontamination governance. The results are
reported per suite and never pooled or averaged across suites. SWE-bench
Pro recorded 2/731 = 0.274\% pass@1 under its original contract, with
all eighteen non-native outcomes retained in the denominator.
VerilogEval recorded 129/312 = 41.35\% pass@1 (67/156 code-completion
and 62/156 spec-to-RTL), with a 129/311 sensitivity figure retained
non-canonically. LAB-Bench's public split was completed at 1,967/1,967
across eight categories, with per-category accuracies ranging from 0.787
for TableQA to 0.000 for CloningScenarios. AgentDojo's benign-utility
scope completed at 97/97. Omni-MATH closed with all 4,428 dispositions
recorded and the official accuracy of 2,271/4,428 = 51.31\% reproduced,
with two parser exclusions verified to have no score effect. The
remaining lanes are recorded as partial, quota-blocked, or externally
blocked: E2 FrontierSWE at 1/17 tasks with a normalised replay of
0.8628, E4 BankerToolBench at 1/100 tasks with a recovery metric of
0.3115, E5 APEX-Agents at 7/480 natively scored with a prefix mean of
0.050505, E9 MLE-bench at 40/75 competitions natively graded with a null
suite score, E13 BALROG at 13/255 episodes, and six lanes (E3, E7,
E8-original, E10-original, E12, E14-original) closed as externally
blocked with no provider response and named reopen conditions.
Structural claims were re-verified by deterministic code checks on 19
September 2026 at 11/11 PASS (source:
\texttt{outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md}). No claim of
improvement over a baseline or over the base model is made anywhere in
the campaign, and the agentic-suite results in particular --- E4's mean
reward of 0.0 across 100 base-model trials, with 27 or more trajectories
ending in role-token degeneration --- are reported as evidence about
tool-dialogue collapse rather than as evidence about the tasks the
suites nominally measure (source:
\texttt{outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md}).

Two gaps in this survey should be stated plainly, since the chapter's
purpose is to locate the work rather than to flatter it. First, the
primary papers for the fourteen evaluation suites are not surveyed here
at the depth the algorithmic literature receives; the suites are
discussed as instruments, sourced to the campaign's own results
document, and their native protocols are documented in Chapter 9 and its
receipt trail rather than in this chapter. Second, the survey's
treatment of tool-use and agentic RL is inherited from the project's own
related-work source and is explicitly flagged there as a concurrent
setting in which the ZVF measurements will need to be re-evaluated
rather than assumed to transfer (source:
\texttt{platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/related\_work\_v2.tex}). What
the chapter does establish is that the project's contribution is a
measurement and reporting contribution, that the literature it sits in
is dense enough that claim-narrowing rather than claim-extension is the
honest move, and that the specific gap the portfolio occupies ---
machine-readable, per-component, executed-loss-form disclosure for
group-relative RL runs --- is not filled by any of the resources
surveyed above.



\chapter{System Requirements
Specification}\label{system-requirements-specification}

This chapter states what the Tinker RL Lab system must do, what
qualities it must hold while doing it, and how each requirement is
verified. It inherits the specification of the Phase-1 report and
extends it across the Semester-4 additions: the multi-framework
benchmark rosters, the Zero-Variance Fraction (ZVF) diagnostic and the
adaptive group-size controller derived from it, the minimum reporting
standard and the machine-readable GRPO stack registry, and the E1--E14
held-out evaluation campaign with its fail-closed completion gate. It is
written as a specification rather than as a retrospective description,
but every requirement below corresponds to a component that exists in
the repository, and every functional requirement is paired with the
concrete test that checks it. Where a requirement is not satisfied ---
the cloud capacity for two evaluation lanes, the white-box gradient path
at the largest model scale --- the shortfall is named in §3.6 and §3.8
rather than written around.

\section{Overview and Actors}\label{overview-and-actors}

The central object of the Phase-1 specification was the \textbf{run}:
one configuration, one seed, one framework, one training budget,
producing per-step telemetry and a persisted set of raw group reward
tensors (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Semester 4 introduces two further first-class objects. The first is the
\textbf{lane}: a single benchmark suite evaluated under a named scope
--- an original contract with the suite's owner, or a declared
replacement scope --- carrying its own provider, payload, licence, quota
and terminal state. The second is the \textbf{receipt}: an immutable,
hash-bound record that a declared step actually happened, against
declared inputs, at a declared revision. Requirements that concern runs,
lanes and receipts are distinguished below where the distinction
matters.

Four actors interact with the system, and the fourth is new in Semester
4.

\textbf{The experimenter} declares work. Under the original design this
meant declaring a run through a single configuration object and nothing
else: the model, the dataset, the reward function, the decoding
parameters (temperature, top-\(p\), maximum tokens), the group size
\(G\), the learning rate, the KL coefficient \(\beta\) and the seed all
live in one file, and the framework is a switch rather than a rewrite
(source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Under the campaign design the same actor declares a lane through a
sealed resource request: the lane specification fixes an immutable task
bundle, a pinned native runtime, a native grader, a budget and an
applicable licence in advance, and those declared inputs are then
checked for internal consistency before any launch is permitted (source:
zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py).

\textbf{The back-end} executes the training loop and, under the managed
path, is the Tinker service; under the white-box path it is a local or
rented GPU. For evaluation, the corresponding actor is the
\textbf{provider grader}: the benchmark owner's own native evaluator at
a pinned revision. The system is permitted to record a suite score only
when that evaluator produced it, or when a declared replacement harness
reproduces it under a scope that is labelled as a replacement (source:
zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py).

\textbf{The auditor} is a downstream script or an independent reader who
recomputes published diagnostics from stored raw artefacts. In Semester
4 this role acquires force: the audit suite of §3.4 is executed as a
gate with a non-zero exit status rather than as advisory tooling, and
the completion gate for the campaign lanes is fail-closed by
construction, in that a missing or malformed declaration stops the
procedure instead of degrading it.

\textbf{The campaign operator} holds launch authority for paid work.
Spend on any lane requires a bound authorization receipt issued by the
operator; the system will not mint one for itself, and lanes are
recorded as blocked when the receipt or the technical chain behind it is
absent (source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).

A fifth party, the \textbf{external access holder}, is an actor only in
the negative sense: several lanes depend on a private payload, a hosted
evaluation service or a quota grant that only the provider can issue.
The specification requires that such a lane be recorded with an explicit
terminal state and a named reopen condition rather than silently
dropped, which is why the campaign's terminal-state vocabulary exists as
a first-class output (source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).

\section{Functional Requirements}\label{functional-requirements}

\textbf{FR-1 --- Single configuration object.} One declarative file
shall fully determine a run: model, dataset, reward function, decoding
parameters, group size, learning rate, KL coefficient, seed and output
location. No framework-specific parameters may be required from the
experimenter to obtain a valid run on any supported back-end (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{FR-2 --- Back-end-agnostic execution through thin adapters.} A
configuration shall be executable on multiple frameworks through
adapters that map the shared configuration onto each framework's own
interface, while sharing the reward grader and the decoding
configuration rather than re-implementing them. Two framework rosters
are in scope and must not be conflated. The cross-RL-library roster
contains TRL, Stable-Baselines3, CleanRL, Tianshou, PufferLib, rl\_games
and d3rlpy; the cross-launcher LLM-RL roster contains Tinker, TRL,
SkyRL, veRL, OpenRLHF, Atropos and the Hugging Face reference launchers
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex).
Completed-run parity across the second roster is a requirement the
system does \textbf{not} yet meet: of its seven entries, only Tinker and
TRL produced completed runs in this release, and the veRL and OpenRLHF
entries in the comparison artefact are dry-run placeholders (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex; source:
platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/framework\_comparison.json).

\textbf{FR-3 --- Per-step signal-starvation measurement.} The training
loop shall compute, at every step, the fraction of prompt-groups whose
within-group reward variance is zero, together with the decomposition of
that fraction into all-correct and all-wrong groups. These are the ZVF
and the collapse decomposition, and they are derived quantities of the
reward tensors rather than proxies (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{FR-4 --- Per-step telemetry.} Each step shall emit a structured
record carrying reward, ZVF, gradient utilisation, the all-correct and
all-wrong collapse counts, policy entropy, completion length and KL
divergence, written both to a machine-readable log and to the experiment
tracker (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Semester-4 controller work extends this record with the per-step
counterfactual comparison between the adaptive schedule and its matched
fixed-group-size control (source:
platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/p5p8/controller\_cf\_per\_step.tsv).

\textbf{FR-5 --- Raw tensor persistence.} The system shall persist the
raw per-group reward tensors for every run, so that any derived
diagnostic can be recomputed deterministically after the fact rather
than trusted from a logged value (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{FR-6 --- Sweeps under a fixed budget.} The runner shall support
sweeps over group size, seed and baseline-versus-intervention arms,
executed under a fixed wall-clock or token budget so that arms are
comparable in compute rather than in step count (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{FR-7 --- White-box gradient path.} For models small enough to
train on a single GPU, the system shall record per-layer LoRA gradient
norms alongside the usual telemetry, so that the relationship between
reward spread and gradient magnitude can be examined directly rather
than inferred. This path is scoped to the small-model configurations and
is not a requirement at the large managed scale, where per-layer
gradients are not exposed (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{FR-8 --- Per-run provenance record.} Every run shall emit a
machine-readable provenance record binding the configuration, the grader
or verifier version, and the rollout hashes, so that a published number
can be tied to the exact inputs that produced it (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
In the campaign, the equivalent obligation is stronger: a lane declares
its provider package and grant documents against published schema
versions, each carrying a 64-character hash, and the declared inputs
must be internally consistent and available locally before the lane is
admissible (source: zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py).

\textbf{FR-9 --- Minimum-reporting manifest and audit.} A run submitted
as a stack-conditioned result shall carry the seven-field minimum-report
manifest --- loss form, reference KL, sampler backend including
base-checkpoint revision and hash, telemetry, group-size schedule,
held-out split, and decontamination --- plus the eighth evaluation item,
a held-out pass@\(k\) report; the resource shall emit a completeness
badge on a 0--100 scale and shall distinguish an unreported field from a
field reported as absent (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P6\_registry.tex; source:
platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/p5p8/minreport\_audit\_summary.json).

\textbf{FR-10 --- Registry query surface.} The stack registry shall be
queryable: a schema plus a set of entry documents and a query interface
that returns entries by stack, field or status, so that a reader can ask
what a given labelled method actually did rather than what its name
implies (source: platform\_hybrid/registry/schema.json; source:
platform\_hybrid/registry/query.py).

\textbf{FR-11 --- Variant-delta records and stack-diff.} For each named
method variant the registry shall store an explicit delta against the
GRPO reference --- for example DAPO's asymmetric clipping, dynamic
sampling, token-level loss, overlong-reward shaping and removed KL term;
Dr.~GRPO's removal of the length and standard-deviation normalisations;
GSPO's sequence-level ratio --- and the stack-diff procedure shall
return a flip-risk verdict on the 0--5 scale when two entries differ in
label but not in substance (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P6\_registry.tex; source:
platform\_hybrid/registry/provenance/).

\textbf{FR-12 --- Adaptive group-size controller.} The system shall
implement a controller that adjusts group size in response to the
measured signal, with an escalation asymmetry, hysteresis to prevent
oscillation, and a callback that diverts degenerate groups away from the
fixed schedule, and it shall record the counterfactual comparison
against the best static recipe at matched and at unequal rollout counts
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P7\_zvf\_controller.tex; source:
platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/p5p8/controller\_cf\_summary.json).
The controller is specified as a training-time policy, not as a
promotion mechanism: the requirement is to measure the trade, not to
claim a win.

\textbf{FR-13 --- Lane harness with evidence classes.} Each evaluation
lane shall be driven by a harness that records per-lane results against
a declared evidence class --- exact complete, partial exact, partial
recovery, externally blocked, or
local-setup-ready-provider-input-required --- and shall keep the class
assignment tied to the source status rather than to the operator's
optimism (source: zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py).

\textbf{FR-14 --- Fail-closed completion gate.} A declared lane package
shall be validated, not executed, by the completion gate. A valid
package proves only that the declared immutable inputs are internally
consistent and available locally; accepting a package as a benchmark
result is explicitly out of scope for the gate. Signature verification
against provider trust roots shall be performed, and the gate shall
refuse rather than warn (source:
zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py).

\textbf{FR-15 --- Scope-separation contract in reporting.}
Original-contract and replacement-scope results shall never be pooled,
no cross-suite aggregate shall be computed, and every reported accuracy
shall carry its coverage denominator. This is a requirement on the
outputs, not only on the analysis code (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md).

\textbf{FR-16 --- Blind-review packaging with an unskippable audit
guard.} The export path shall produce an anonymised review package whose
build runs the audit suite, and the guard shall detect and refuse a
package built with the audits skipped (source:
platform\_local/run\_all\_audits.py; source:
platform\_local/export\_guard\_audit.py).

\section{Non-Functional Requirements}\label{non-functional-requirements}

\textbf{NFR-1 --- Attribution.} Every comparative claim shall be made
with the whole stack held fixed except the factor under test, and
framework, algorithm, model family and scale shall be reported as
confounded where they are confounded (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex).

\textbf{NFR-2 --- Reproducibility.} Multi-arm claims shall rest on at
least three seeds; single-seed results shall be labelled descriptive or
exploratory and shall not be used to support a directional claim
(source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{NFR-3 --- Recomputability.} Every published derived diagnostic
shall be reproducible from stored raw artefacts by a documented
procedure, with no dependence on a logged summary value (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{NFR-4 --- Honesty and denominator discipline.} Held-out figures
shall be reported with their denominator \(n\); negative and null
results shall be reported as such; where an analysis was underpowered,
that shall be stated rather than left implicit (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/main\_eai\_body.tex).

\textbf{NFR-5 --- Isolation.} Concurrent experiments shall run in
separate processes rather than threads, following a tracker
thread-safety defect observed in Phase 1 (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{NFR-6 --- Budget.} Managed-compute spend shall be recorded per
run and counted against a declared envelope; even where the standing
authorization removes the cumulative cap and sets the total to
unlimited, per-run resource and timeout bounds remain in force and paid
launches remain bound to a lead-issued authorization receipt (source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).

\textbf{NFR-7 --- Fail-closed behaviour.} A validation boundary shall
default to refusal. The gate neither invokes an adapter nor accepts a
package as a result, and a package that cannot be verified is not scored
(source: zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py).

\textbf{NFR-8 --- Least privilege and no credential retention.} The
system shall hold no key material in its evidence records: credential
checks record only liveness and scope, and the resulting receipts state
explicitly that no key material is stored (source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).

\textbf{NFR-9 --- Auditability under lost-source conditions.} Execution
sources for anything declared sealed shall live in tracked paths, so
that a lost working directory cannot orphan a sealed artefact; where
loss nonetheless occurs, the sealed requests and receipts shall remain
sufficient to pin a faithful re-implementation (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md).

\textbf{NFR-10 --- Traceability of external artefacts.} Externally
supplied payloads and hosted results shall be bound by hash equality and
validated against published trust roots and schema versions, and an
unreported field shall be represented as a null rather than as a zero or
a false value (source:
zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py; source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P6\_registry.tex).

\section{Test-Case Specification}\label{test-case-specification}

The test cases below are the concrete checks attached to the
requirements. TC-1 through TC-5 are inherited from the Phase-1
specification and are restated here because later chapters depend on
them; TC-6 onward are the Semester-4 additions, and TC-6 is the suite
that carries the largest share of the functional requirements.

\textbf{TC-1 --- Recompute ZVF and the collapse decomposition from
stored tensors.} Recompute the published ZVF and collapse figures from
the persisted per-group reward tensors of the main configuration, using
the documented analysis script. The published values are a ZVF of
approximately 0.72--0.77 and an all-correct fraction of 0.65--0.71;
recomputation must match (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Verifies FR-3 and FR-5.

\textbf{TC-2 --- Recompute the detector metric from raw tensors.}
Recompute the Phase-3 detector's area under the ROC curve from raw
tensors under five-fold stratified cross-validation. The published
figure is an AUROC of \(0.84 \pm 0.01\) against \(0.43\) for the
reward-only comparator (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Verifies FR-5 and NFR-3.

\textbf{TC-3 --- Re-run a matched-budget arm comparison.} Re-run the
baseline and the curriculum arm at a matched token budget over three
seeds; the Phase-1 result is that both arms average \(+0.028\) on the
held-out set with no curriculum advantage (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Verifies FR-1, FR-2 and FR-6.

\textbf{TC-4 --- Recompute headline diagnostics.} Recompute each
headline diagnostic quoted in a results chapter directly from stored
artefacts, with the recomputation recorded next to the published value
(source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Verifies NFR-3.

\textbf{TC-5 --- Provenance verification.} Verify a run's provenance
record end to end, from configuration to rollout hashes to the grader
version (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Verifies FR-8.

\textbf{TC-6 --- Execute the submission audit suite as a gate.} Run
\texttt{python\ platform\_local/run\_all\_audits.py}. The suite executes
nine audits --- \texttt{claim\_issues}, \texttt{sync\_issues},
\texttt{anon\_issues}, \texttt{strength\_issues},
\texttt{package\_issues}, \texttt{workflow\_issues},
\texttt{export\_guard\_issues}, \texttt{caveat\_issues} and
\texttt{scientific\_issues} --- emits one
\texttt{METRIC\ \textless{}name\textgreater{}=\textless{}count\textgreater{}}
line per audit, and returns exit status 0 only when every audit reports
zero issues (source: platform\_local/run\_all\_audits.py; source:
utils/audit\_utils.py). Individual checks that this test enforces
include the presence of scope and caveat language in the review
documents (\texttt{readme\_missing\_key\_results\_scope\_header},
\texttt{readme\_missing\_heldout\_language},
\texttt{checklist\_missing\_preliminary\_key\_results\_label}), the
absence of overstated checkpoint claims
(\texttt{misleading\_checkpoint\_availability\_claim},
\texttt{submission\_overstates\_checkpoint\_release}), the presence of
every reviewer caveat (\texttt{heldout\_scope},
\texttt{tool\_eval\_protocol}, \texttt{codegen\_subset},
\texttt{capacity\_confound}, \texttt{moe\_diagnostics},
\texttt{budget\_and\_splits}, \texttt{replication\_release}), the
absence of banned marketing formulations (source:
platform\_local/claim\_strength\_audit.py), the survival of the
technical caveats into the anonymous build
(\texttt{anonymous\_missing:\textless{}label\textgreater{}} for the
seven needles), and the presence of the audit call and its skip flag in
the export script (source: platform\_local/paper\_sync\_audit.py;
source: platform\_local/export\_guard\_audit.py). Verifies FR-16, NFR-4
and NFR-7.

\textbf{TC-7 --- Structural checks on the evaluation script and its
result files.} For each held-out evaluation script, assert by parsing
the source --- not by reading its documentation --- that it exposes a
seed argument, locks the dataset split to the test split, samples with a
non-zero temperature, consumes a checkpoint path, and writes the
declared metadata keys including the split, the sampling flag, the seed
and the model source. For each result file, assert that the schema
version is 2, that the evaluation status is one of completed or failed,
that the required configuration and summary fields are present, that the
recorded split is the test split, that the attempted count equals the
sum of correct and incorrect, and that a failed evaluation carries a
failure reason and a completed evaluation carries non-zero attempts
(source: platform\_local/scientific\_audit.py). Verifies FR-8 and NFR-4.

\textbf{TC-8 --- Build the report from source.} Compile the main report
and its supplementary through the full LaTeX chain and flag an empty
journal field in the bibliography (source:
platform\_local/scientific\_audit.py). Verifies FR-16.

\textbf{TC-9 --- Dry-run the completion gate against a declared lane.}
Present a provider package to the gate and require it to validate the
declaration without executing anything: the expected outcomes are
acceptance with the declared evidence class, or a specific refusal
naming the missing element. A package must never be reported as a
benchmark result by this procedure (source:
zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py). Verifies FR-13,
FR-14 and NFR-7.

\textbf{TC-10 --- Registry query and null discipline.} Query the
registry for a labelled entry and its delta record, and confirm that a
field absent from the source is returned as unreported rather than as a
zero value, and that a same-label pair with divergent behaviour triggers
the flip-risk verdict (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P6\_registry.tex; source:
platform\_hybrid/registry/query.py). Verifies FR-9, FR-10, FR-11 and
NFR-10.

Two rows deserve comment. FR-7 has no automated check because the
white-box gradient path is a measurement procedure rather than an
invariant; its evidence is the result data itself. NFR-5 is likewise
checked operationally rather than by the suite, because process
isolation is a property of how runs are launched and is not visible in
any post-hoc artefact.

\section{Hardware Requirements}\label{hardware-requirements}

The system requires four compute surfaces. The \textbf{managed training
back-end} executes GRPO at the scale of the campaign actor, a
mixture-of-experts model of roughly 35 billion total parameters with
about 3 billion active per token, evaluated in bfloat16 with a LoRA
adapter at a pinned revision (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md). The requirement here is
not a particular accelerator but the service contract: the back-end
exposes sampling, training and checkpointing, and does \textbf{not}
expose per-layer gradients, which is why FR-7 is scoped to the
small-model path (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

The \textbf{white-box GPU} requirement is a single CUDA device with at
least 24 GB of memory, met in Phase 1 by a cloud notebook instance with
an L4-class GPU, sufficient for the 1.5-billion-parameter configuration
and for a scaled re-test at 3 billion parameters on reduced sequence
lengths (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
The \textbf{batch scoring and held-out evaluation} surface is a
serverless GPU path used for large scoring sweeps and for the hosted
sampler bridge that the tool-use lane depends on; that bridge is a
required component, and its availability is a precondition for that lane
rather than an assumption (source:
outputs/UNBLOCK\_CARRYOUT\_2026-09-21.md).

The \textbf{evaluation-lane compute} is the Semester-4 addition with the
sharpest unmet requirement. Two lanes are gated on cloud capacity that
is currently below the required level: one needs at least eight standard
on-demand vCPUs in its region against a current grant of one of eight,
and the other needs at least sixteen against a current grant of one of
sixteen, with quota increase requests filed and still open (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md; source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).
A third lane requires a local container build for which the recipe
reconstruction needs at least 30 GiB of free disk against 31 GiB
available, which is marginal rather than comfortable (source:
outputs/UNBLOCK\_CARRYOUT\_2026-09-21.md). A fourth lane requires Cloud
project resources whose binding must be minted by the project owner;
that binding is a hard prerequisite the system cannot satisfy on its own
(source: outputs/UNBLOCK\_CARRYOUT\_2026-09-21.md). The
\textbf{orchestration workspace} is an ordinary developer workstation
used for orchestration, analysis and figure generation, and carries the
audit suite and the report toolchain.

\section{Software Requirements}\label{software-requirements}

The runtime is Python 3.11 with PyTorch and the Hugging Face stack
(Transformers, PEFT, Datasets) for model handling, tokenisation and
adapters; the RL back-ends are TRL, veRL through its HybridFlow path,
and OpenRLHF, driven through the shared launcher, with the Tinker client
SDK for the managed path (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Experiment tracking is a weights-and-biases project; telemetry is stored
as JSONL and as persisted tensors; analysis is carried out by the
documented scripts named in the Phase-1 specification (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

Semester 4 adds five software components to the requirement set. First,
the audit suite: \texttt{platform\_local/run\_all\_audits.py} together
with the nine audit modules it imports and the shared data model in
\texttt{utils/audit\_utils.py}, which defines the issue, result and
suite types and the \texttt{METRIC} line format; the suite requires a
Python interpreter with no third-party dependencies beyond the standard
library for its structural checks (source:
platform\_local/run\_all\_audits.py; source: utils/audit\_utils.py).
Second, the completion gate and the per-lane harnesses under
\texttt{zvf-program/}, which require Ed25519 signature verification and
JSON Schema validation against the published provider-package,
provider-grant and trust-root schema versions (source:
zvf-program/flagship/e1\_e14\_completion\_gate.py). Third, the registry:
a schema, an entry store and a query interface, together with the
provenance records under \texttt{platform\_hybrid/registry/} (source:
platform\_hybrid/registry/query.py). Fourth, the report toolchain: a
LaTeX distribution capable of the multi-pass build with BibTeX, plus a
markdown assembler that concatenates the chapter files and, when
available, renders the document through pandoc (source:
platform\_local/scientific\_audit.py; source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Third\_Review\_2026-09-24/thesis/assemble\_thesis.py).
Fifth, the judgement-layer harness under \texttt{zvf-program/jev\_lab/},
which records its own receipts and is used only for classification,
faithfulness checking and value ordering (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md).

\section{Constraints and Assumptions}\label{constraints-and-assumptions}

\textbf{Rewards are binary and verifiable.} The specification assumes a
programmatic checker --- exact match for the mathematics tasks and unit
tests for the code subset. Nothing in the requirement set addresses
learned reward models, and none of the diagnostics should be read as
applying to them (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{Held-out sets are small.} Several evaluation sets have \(n\)
between 8 and 20, with the consequence that learning-gain magnitudes are
noise-limited by construction. This is a constraint on what the results
can mean, not a defect of the analysis: the small-\(n\) figures are
reported with their denominators and are not used to support comparative
claims (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{The managed back-end is a black box at the layer level.}
Per-layer gradients are not exposed on the managed path, so claims about
the relationship between reward spread and gradient magnitude are
confined to the small-model white-box configuration and are directional
only (source: platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P7\_zvf\_controller.tex).

\textbf{Group-size differences must not be attributed to sampling
noise.} The comparison protocols share a deterministic prompt schedule,
so that method-to-method differences in ZVF reflect training dynamics
rather than independent draws of prompts (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

\textbf{Framework parity is incomplete.} The requirement in FR-2 covers
two seven-library rosters, but only two of the seven cross-launcher
entries produced completed runs in this release, and the remaining
comparison entries are dry-run placeholders. Comparative framework
claims are therefore constrained to the rosters and the completed runs
actually recorded, and the repository states the dry-run status
explicitly (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex; source:
platform\_hybrid/experiments/results/framework\_comparison.json).

\textbf{The scale study reports descriptive limits rather than a scaling
law.} The cross-scale analysis spans more than seventy runs across seven
libraries and five model families, and its central result is a negative
one: no reliable positive cross-scale slope is recovered and no
saturation exponent is identifiable. The specification inherits that
boundary --- the requirement is to report identifiability limits, not to
assert a law (source: platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P1\_scaling.tex).

\textbf{External dependencies are assumed unavailable until proven
otherwise.} Payload grants, hosted evaluations and quota increases are
treated as absent until a response arrives; a lane with no provider
response is closed as externally blocked with a reopen condition rather
than left pending indefinitely (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md).

\textbf{Arithmetic and string lookups stay in code.} Whatever judgement
layer is used for triage, classification or value ordering,
verification-type checks are performed by deterministic code. This rule
was banked after the judgement layer returned an incorrect answer to a
parity probe in a healthy window, and it constrains the architecture: no
requirement in this chapter may be verified by a language model where a
deterministic check is possible (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md).

\textbf{Named variants are labels, not specifications.} The assumption
that a method name identifies a method is false on the available
evidence: entries sharing a label diverge substantially in measured
behaviour, which is why the registry's delta records and flip-risk
verdicts are specified as requirements rather than as documentation
niceties (source: platform\_hybrid/paper/paper\_P6\_registry.tex).



\part{Part II --- Design and Implementation}


\chapter{Proposed Methodology}\label{proposed-methodology}


\fig{fig_four_pillars}
{The four de-confound pillars. Each holds the whole stack fixed and varies exactly one factor.}
{fig:pillars}



This chapter sets out the methodology of the work rather than its
results. It states four things in order: the design commitment to
attribution and the four de-confound pillars that operationalise it
(§4.1); the per-step telemetry that makes a run's internal state
observable at the granularity at which the mechanism acts (§4.2); the
formalisation of the Zero-Variance Fraction as a descriptive diagnostic
rather than a metric (§4.3); the eight-study portfolio P1--P8 and the
question each study asks (§4.4); and the design of the E1--E14 held-out
evaluation campaign, including its single trained actor, pinned native
evaluators, frozen sampling, receipt model, terminal-state taxonomy and
decontamination handling (§4.5). Throughout, the reporting discipline is
part of the method: figures are given only where a repository file
supports them, and where a lane produced no admissible result, it is
recorded as blocked rather than written around.

\section{The Design Commitment to
Attribution}\label{the-design-commitment-to-attribution}


\fig{fig_attribution}
{The attribution design. Exactly one factor of the training stack is varied while the remainder is held fixed, so a measured difference cannot be attributed to an unstated framework difference.}
{fig:attribution}



The premise of the project is that an RL-for-LLM result is not
identified by the name of its algorithm. Two runs both labelled GRPO may
differ in loss form, reference-policy handling, sampler, precision,
base-checkpoint identity, data order and group-size schedule, and the
label alone does not say which of those differences produced the
outcome. The project's response is a design commitment rather than a
post-hoc covariate adjustment: hold the whole experimental stack fixed
and vary exactly one factor at a time, so that an observed difference is
attributable to the factor that was varied rather than to the several
things that changed alongside it (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
This commitment is the methodological spine of the whole thesis, and it
has a practical corollary that shapes every experiment reported later: a
study is defined by the single axis it varies and is only interpretable
to the extent that every other axis was genuinely pinned.

The commitment is implemented in a unified launcher,
\texttt{platform\_local/unified/launcher.py}, which consumes a single
configuration object and dispatches it to thin per-framework adapters
--- \texttt{trl\_integrations/}, \texttt{verl/},
\texttt{platform\_modal/openrlhf/}, \texttt{platform\_hybrid/skyrl/} and
\texttt{platform\_tinker/atropos/} --- with the reference GRPO loop
implemented once in \texttt{platform\_tinker/tinkerrl/grpo.py} (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
The reward grader and the decoding path are shared code. That sharing is
the load-bearing part of the design: because the grader and the sampler
are the same objects across back-ends, a difference in outcome between
two frameworks is attributable to the framework, and not to a divergent
reward parser or a differently configured decoder masquerading as an
algorithmic difference. The commitment also bounds what a result may
claim. A completed run supports a statement about that stack, at that
revision, with that grader; it does not license a statement about
``GRPO'' in the abstract, and the project's reporting standard (§4.4,
P5) is built around that distinction.

Four pillars carry the commitment, each holding the remainder of the
stack fixed while varying one factor (source: platform\_hybrid/sem 4
work/submissions/mtech-final-review/build\_review\_pptx.py, slide 7):

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}@{}}
\caption{The four de-confound pillars, the single factor each isolates,
and the design that holds the remainder fixed.}\tabularnewline
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Pillar
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Factor isolated
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Design
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endfirsthead
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Pillar
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Factor isolated
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Design
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
1 · PPO versus GRPO, same stack & Advantage estimator only & Identical
model, data and stack \\
2 · Zero-Variance Fraction & Signal starvation and its confounders &
Per-step ZVF measured across nine libraries \\
3 · Trainability and group size & Contrast density against the variance
knob & G ∈ \{2, 4, 8, 16\} × seeds, held out \\
4 · Held-out generalisation & Whether GRPO adds real capability &
Dr.~GRPO versus GRPO, pre-to-post, McNemar \\
\end{longtable}

Pillar 1 asks whether a reported GRPO advantage survives when the
estimator is the only thing that changes; on the measured Qwen3-8B
comparison the paired difference is small and not statistically
significant (paired Δ = −0.002, p = 0.374; source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/frontier\_synthesis\_scaling.tex, backed
by \texttt{samestack\_ppo\_grpo.json}), while the same-stack Welch test
for that comparison is recorded in the historical twenty-test family at
p = 0.7605 and flagged there as a provenance conflict rather than an
estimate (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex). Pillar 2 turns a
run's internal signal state into a first-class measurement; it is
formalised in §4.3. Pillar 3 varies the group size G while holding
model, data, reward and schedule fixed, and is the axis on which the
preference-density argument of P3 rests. Pillar 4 asks the question the
other three cannot answer --- whether any of this changes held-out
behaviour --- by comparing a pre-RL checkpoint with its post-RL
successor on data the training loop never saw.

Held fixed across the experiment programme are the training protocol
constants: LoRA rank 32; Adam with β₁ = 0.9 and β₂ = 0.95; learning rate
1 × 10⁻⁴ for the headline configuration; and the seed set \{42, 123,
456, 789, 1024\} (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/neurips\_2026\_variants/paper\_P8\_workshop.tex).
The model roster spans the Qwen3, Qwen3.5, Llama-3.x, DeepSeek-V3.1 and
Nemotron-120B families, from 0.6B to approximately 671B total
parameters, including mixture-of-experts anchors at the frontier end of
that range (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/neurips\_2026\_variants/paper\_P8\_workshop.tex).
The statistical apparatus is fixed in advance of the comparisons it
supports: Welch t-tests and Mann--Whitney U tests for two-sample
contrasts; Cohen's d with Hedges--Olkin intervals; 95\% bootstrap
confidence intervals with 10,000 resamples; rliable aggregate metrics
(interquartile mean, median and optimality gap); and Bonferroni and
Benjamini--Hochberg corrections over a declared test family (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex). Power was
computed rather than assumed: at 80\% power the minimum detectable
effect for an equal-n comparison of five seeds against five is d\_min =
2.024, improving to d\_min = 1.530 when a five-seed group is compared
against a pooled group of fifteen (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex). Those figures
are a standing reminder of how coarse a five-seed design is, and the
project reports several null results that are null precisely because the
design could not have detected anything smaller.

Two limitations belong in this section rather than in a later
discussion. First, the framework roster is broader than the framework
evidence: although seven RL libraries are named in the study design,
only Tinker and TRL produced completed runs, and the veRL and OpenRLHF
entries in \texttt{framework\_comparison.json} are dry-run placeholders
rather than measurements (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex). Cross-library
claims are therefore narrower than the roster implies. Second, the
variance decomposition that motivates the whole attribution commitment
is itself a one-way analysis over 42 experiments with valid performance
observations, where library/framework explains η² = 0.546 and training
algorithm η² = 0.558, ahead of model family at η² = 0.471 and model
scale at η² = 0.322 (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex). The historical
twenty-test multiple-comparison family that accompanies it pre-dates the
Tier-A/B/C partition, silently includes single-seed rows whose Welch
statistics are mathematically undefined, and reduces to a single
surviving comparison under the restricted family; it is retained as a
reproducibility audit trail and explicitly not as a corrected survival
claim (source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/\_shared\_methods.tex).

\section{Per-Step Telemetry}\label{per-step-telemetry}


\fig{fig_telemetry}
{Per-step telemetry recorded by the unified harness, and the destinations each record flows to.}
{fig:telemetry}



Attribution is only as good as the run's observability. Every training
step therefore emits a record containing the Zero-Variance Fraction;
gradient utilisation; the collapse decomposition that separates
all-correct from all-wrong zero-variance groups; and, alongside them,
reward, loss, entropy, completion length and the KL divergence to the
reference policy (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
Records are written to JSONL and mirrored to Weights \& Biases, so a run
can be analysed locally without a network dependency and reviewed
remotely without a local environment. The reason ZVF and its
decomposition are logged as separate fields rather than collapsed into a
single starvation number is that the same aggregate fraction can
describe opposite situations: a group of completions that are all
correct and a group that are all wrong both contribute zero within-group
variance, and the remedy for each is different. Logging the
decomposition preserves that distinction at the point of collection
instead of trying to recover it afterwards.

The quantity being logged is the group-relative advantage actually used
by the runner,

A\_i = (r\_i − μ\_g) / (σ\_g + ε), μ\_g = (1/G) Σ\_j r\_j, σ\_g =
sqrt((1/G) Σ\_j (r\_j − μ\_g)²)

(source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
When σ\_g = 0 every A\_i is zero, so the whole group contributes no
gradient through the advantage (surrogate) term. The parenthetical
qualification matters and is preserved throughout this thesis: it is the
advantage term that vanishes, not necessarily the total update, because
the KL penalty and any auxiliary loss or clipping term are unaffected
(source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).

Two further design choices distinguish this telemetry from conventional
training curves. The first is that the raw per-group reward tensors are
retained, not only the summaries computed from them. This makes a
published number auditable by recomputation: any downstream diagnostic,
such as \texttt{p2\_collapse\_analysis.py}, can reproduce a reported
statistic from the stored tensors rather than trusting a logged summary
(source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
The convention has been exercised. Study P2 recomputed three stored
GSM8K group tensors from their raw values and obtained 0.130, 0.190 and
0.155, pooling to 0.1583 --- figures reproduced from the tensors rather
than read back from a log line (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p2\_abstract.tex). The second is that
the logging layer is treated as part of the apparatus and is expected to
fail. An early Weights \& Biases integration used the library's
thread-unsafe global run object from multiple worker threads, which
produced repeated ``Run is finished'' summary-write failures; the fix
was to give each run its own process in \texttt{campaign.py} (source:
platform\_tinker/reports/esa\_phase1/Phase1\_Project\_Report\_ZVF.tex).
The defect is recorded because telemetry that silently drops writes is
indistinguishable from telemetry showing a healthy run, and the remedy
--- process-level isolation per run --- is a structural property of the
campaign rather than a patch.

\section{Formalising the Zero-Variance
Fraction}\label{formalising-the-zero-variance-fraction}


\fig{fig_gradient_utilisation}
{Gradient utilisation, $1-\zvf$, as a function of group size $G$. Increasing $G$ reduces the zero-variance fraction with saturating benefit, which is why larger groups are not uniformly better.}
{fig:gu}




\fig{fig_zvf_definition}
{Definition of the Zero-Variance Fraction. One prompt produces $G$ completions with scalar rewards; when the within-group reward standard deviation is zero the advantage is identically zero and the sample contributes no gradient. ZVF is the fraction of such groups.}
{fig:zvfdef}



The Zero-Variance Fraction is the share of prompt groups in a batch
whose reward variance is zero. Formally, for a batch B\_t of prompt
groups at step t, each group g contributing K sampled completions with
rewards r\_\{g,1\}, \ldots, r\_\{g,K\},

ZVF\_t = (1 / \textbar B\_t\textbar) Σ\_\{g ∈ B\_t\} 1{[}
Var̂(r\_\{g,1\}, \ldots, r\_\{g,K\}) ≤ ε {]}

where Var̂ is the unbiased sample variance and ε = 10⁻⁶ for rewards
normalised to {[}0, 1{]} (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex). A
prompt-set formulation used elsewhere in the paper sources writes the
same quantity as ZVF\_t = (1 / \textbar P\_t\textbar) Σ\_\{p ∈ P\_t\}
1{[} Var\_\{c \textasciitilde{} C(p)\} r(c) = 0 {]}, with C(p) the set
of sampled completions for prompt p (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/neurips\_2026\_variants/paper\_P8\_workshop.tex).
The reference implementation is deliberately small, so that the
diagnostic cannot acquire hidden behaviour:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ zvf(rewards\_2d: np.ndarray, eps: }\BuiltInTok{float} \OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{1e{-}6}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}\textgreater{}} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{:}
    \CommentTok{\# rewards\_2d shape: [num\_groups, K]}
\NormalTok{    var\_per\_group }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ rewards\_2d.var(axis}\OperatorTok{={-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, ddof}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{((var\_per\_group }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=}\NormalTok{ eps).mean())}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/zvf\_pipeline\_spec.tex).
Gradient utilisation is then defined as its complement, GU\_t = 1 −
ZVF\_t (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/neurips\_2026\_variants/paper\_P8\_workshop.tex).
The complement is a descriptive accounting identity, not a claim about
optimiser behaviour: when ZVF\_t = 1, every prompt has zero centred
reward-contrast coefficient in this group-standardised runner, and the
source is explicit that this ``does not assert a zero total gradient for
objectives with KL, auxiliary losses, clipping, or token masking''
(source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/neurips\_2026\_variants/paper\_P8\_workshop.tex).

Three properties make the definition more than a restatement of the
training loop. First, it is non-tautological under a simple null: if
completions were independent Bernoulli draws with per-prompt success
probability p\_g, then E{[}ZVF\_t{]} = E\_\{p\_g\}{[}p\_g\^{}K + (1 −
p\_g)\^{}K{]}, a quantity that depends on the model's accuracy
distribution and the group size rather than on the observed batch
(source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex). The
identity pass@G − p\^{}G = 1 − ZVF follows algebraically under the same
i.i.d.-Bernoulli model and is therefore reported as an algebraic remark
rather than as evidence (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p2\_abstract.tex). Second, it was
validated against that null on instrumented runs: a Qwen2.5-0.5B sweep
over G ∈ \{2, 4, 8, 16\}, three seeds and 40 steps --- twelve runs and
480 logged steps --- found per-step observed ZVF correlating at r = 0.92
with the Bernoulli-null prediction p\^{}G + (1 − p)\^{}G, with mean ZVF
of 0.84, 0.76, 0.69 and 0.63 at G = 2, 4, 8 and 16 respectively (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex).
Third, at frontier scale the same instrument was run on Qwen3-8B against
held-out GSM8K through the Tinker managed API at G = 8, temperature 1.0,
over 200 problems and three seeds, yielding a mean ZVF of 0.16 composed
of 12.7\% all-correct groups, 3.2\% all-wrong groups and 84\% mixed
groups; the raw correlation with the mechanical null is approximately r
= 0.93 with rank ρ ≈ 0.65, and the i.i.d. null over-predicts
(approximately 0.28 against the observed 0.16), which is the expected
direction when the reward signal is real rather than noise (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex).

That the quantity is portable across libraries is a separate, weaker
claim, and the project states its limits explicitly. A normaliser at
\texttt{scripts/zvf\_compute\_cross\_framework.py} maps each framework's
reward and mask fields onto the common definition --- TRL's
\texttt{rewards} with \texttt{batch\_size\ /\ group\_size} and
\texttt{completion\_mask}; Tinker's \texttt{rollout.reward} with
\texttt{rollout.group\_id} and \texttt{rollout.eos\_mask}; OpenRLHF's
\texttt{reward\_score} with \texttt{prompt\_index} and
\texttt{attention\_mask}; veRL's \texttt{data\_source/reward} with
\texttt{uid} and \texttt{response\_mask} --- and emits JSONL records of
the form \texttt{\{step,\ zvf,\ batch\_reward\_mean,\ n\_groups,\ K\}}
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/zvf\_pipeline\_spec.tex). The
scope correction attached to that parser is that it normalises reward
logs and does not make its runner canonical GRPO; because the project
does not bundle raw per-group rollout JSONL for every framework, no
fresh four-way pointwise ZVF overlay is claimed, only the narrower
implementation-level portability claim (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/zvf\_pipeline\_spec.tex). Sensitivity to
the threshold ε is bounded: the reported ε-sensitivity is at most 0.4\%
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/zvf\_pipeline\_spec.tex).
Boundary conditions are declared where the diagnostic does not apply:
dense or continuous reward signals, K = 1, SFT-saturated baselines and
non-outcome-reward RL all fall outside the setting in which the
definition is meaningful (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex).

The methodological point of this section is that ZVF is a diagnostic and
not a metric, and the project defends that position with its own
negative evidence rather than with a general caution. Four observations
support it. First, the same ZVF value can describe mastery or
incapacity: in a 368-run Weights \& Biases audit across seven models,
ZVF sits at 0.95--0.97 at both reward extremes and at 0.25--0.29 at
mid-range rewards of 0.35--0.50, so a high value is as consistent with a
solved task as with an unsolved one (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex). Second, the pooled
association between ZVF and performance, Pearson r = −0.769 (p = 0.0008)
with Spearman ρ = −0.784 (p = 0.0005) over N = 15 experiments spanning
five model families, is confounded by task type: tool-use experiments
reach ZVF = 100\% at approximately 0\% reward while GSM8K experiments
average ZVF = 8.5\%, and within GSM8K the association weakens to r =
+0.40 (p = 0.09, N = 19) and then to r = +0.13 (p = 0.79) when
restricted to the seven model-varying runs (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/neurips\_2026\_variants/paper\_P8\_workshop.tex).
A statistic whose sign is set by which tasks are included in the pool is
not a score. Third, ZVF does not track final accuracy (r ≈ 0.09, n =
12), and P2 reports only a weak association with final held-out outcome
(ρ ≈ 0.27) while deliberately declining to promote ZVF to a causal or
incrementally predictive statistic (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex;
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p2\_abstract.tex). Fourth, no step-level
p-value is computed on the 480 autocorrelated steps of the instrumented
sweep, because treating correlated within-run steps as independent
samples would manufacture significance (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/appendix\_zvf\_formalization.tex). One
further boundary is recorded for the same reason: a variance-mitigation
table in the paper sources is a declared simulation projection and not
evidence, and its rows should be read as synthetic rather than as
independent library deployments (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p2\_abstract.tex;
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/zvf.tex). The result of these choices is
a diagnostic that is useful for describing where within a batch the
learning signal is and is not, and that is explicitly not proposed as a
quantity to be maximised, ranked or reported as a headline score.

\section{The Eight-Study Portfolio}\label{the-eight-study-portfolio}

Semester 3 delivered the shared code and the initial experimental
foundation; the Semester 4 programme --- the research direction, the
expanded experiments, the analysis and the P1--P8 papers --- is the
present author's solo continuation, and the roster records 206 analysis
iterations behind the numbers reported in this thesis (source:
platform\_hybrid/sem 4 work/README.md). The portfolio is organised as
eight studies, each asking one question and each carrying its own claim
boundary. The questions and boundaries are stated here because they are
methodological commitments: a study's question determines which
comparisons it may make, and the boundary states what it declines to
conclude.

P1, ``Scaling Laws for GRPO Post-Training of Language Models: A
Cross-Library, Cross-Scale Study'', asks whether GRPO post-training
outcomes scale with model scale in a way that can be fitted and
extrapolated (source: platform\_hybrid/sem 4 work/README.md;
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p1\_abstract.tex). Its answer is a
conservative negative one: across more than seventy runs and seven RL
libraries over five model families from 0.6B to 1T parameters, the
cross-scale slope is flat over roughly 2.4 orders of magnitude; four of
five frontier-scale traces have their saturation rate pinned at the fit
boundary and are unidentifiable; the internal three-phase geometric
check matches only 2 of 12 anchors; and the Nemotron-120B trace shows a
zero-reward step fraction of 0.55 against at most 0.067 elsewhere
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p1\_abstract.tex). The frontier
anchors --- Qwen3-235B-MoE, DeepSeek-V3.1, Kimi-K2 and Nemotron-120B ---
were run through the Tinker managed API in a single-seed,
descriptive-only regime, and the paper's claim boundary is a descriptive
limits-and-identifiability audit rather than a positive scaling law;
matched multi-seed cross-scale runs are an explicit non-claim (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p1\_abstract.tex).

P2, ``The Zero-Variance Fraction: A Descriptive Diagnostic for Signal
Starvation in GRPO'', asks how much of a training batch carries usable
group-relative signal, and whether that fraction predicts the final
outcome (source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p2\_abstract.tex). The
study characterises ZVF descriptively, establishes the algebraic
identity of §4.3 as algebraic rather than empirical, reports the weak
association with final held-out outcome (ρ ≈ 0.27), and marks the
variance-mitigation table as a simulation projection; the retired roster
item that previously carried a causal framing now lives under
\texttt{archive/absorbed/R02\_main\_zvf/} (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p2\_abstract.tex).

P3, ``Group Size in GRPO: Contrast Density and the Bridge to DPO'', asks
how trainability varies with the group size G and whether GRPO admits an
all-pairs preference interpretation (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p3\_abstract.tex). Two results anchor
it. The first is that trainability varies non-monotonically with G, with
intermediate sizes often outperforming both the smallest and the largest
settings, although the data do not justify a universal optimum; measured
equivalence tests extend to G = 16, retained held-out accuracy is
essentially flat across G ∈ \{2, \ldots, 16\} on the near-ceiling
arithmetic task with a retention ratio of approximately 1.00 between G =
2 and G = 16 (a ratio, not an accuracy above 100\%), and effective
signal-to-noise rises only sublinearly with G, at approximately 52\% of
the √G ideal; no directly trained matched-budget G = 32 cell exists, and
the G = 32 values come from an explicitly labelled reconstructed
token-budget grid and are hypotheses rather than measurements (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p3\_abstract.tex). A 505-task
conditional utility audit using (1 − ZVF)/√G selects G = 4 over G = 5 by
0.00177 with a 95\% bootstrap interval of {[}0.00082, 0.00270{]},
conditional on cohort and objective (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p3\_abstract.tex). The second is that
GRPO admits an all-pairs preference interpretation resembling DPO,
offered as an interpretation and not as a proven equivalence (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p3\_abstract.tex).

P4, ``Length Bias and Held-Out Generalization in GRPO and Dr.~GRPO'',
asks whether the verbosity trap associated with GRPO appears in the
measured regime and whether either method changes held-out accuracy
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p4\_abstract.tex). The headline
is a carefully scoped negative: in the short-horizon GSM8K
chain-of-thought regime with a 200-token generation cap, the mean
Qwen3-8B GRPO gain over its pre-RL checkpoint on held-out data is small
and not statistically significant, and GRPO and Dr.~GRPO are
indistinguishable at the seed budget used; neither inflates length
within the capped regime, with pooled mean completion length drifting
from 194.4 to 183.5 tokens. The 200-token cap bounds the claim, and the
absence of the verbosity signature is the expected outcome when length
is already controlled rather than a positive finding (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p4\_abstract.tex).

P5, ``Report the Stack, Not the Label: RL-for-LLM Results Are
Stack-Conditioned'', asks how much of a reported result is attributable
to the stack rather than to the algorithm label, and what a reportable
stack must contain (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p5\_abstract.tex). Three exhibits carry
the argument: a nominally matched-configuration backend audit spanning
final training rewards from 5.0\% to 85.6\%, a seventeen-fold same-label
swing, in which the managed backend also silently pinned a different
base checkpoint --- undisclosed bundling being part of the exhibit
rather than a footnote to it; a ``DAPO'' comparison in which true
dynamic sampling on an open trainer produced a mean ZVF of 0.00 while
the same label approximated on a closed stack through an asymmetric-clip
surrogate produced a mean ZVF of 0.58; and a twelve-cell head-to-head of
four labelled algorithms on one fixed stack landing within a 0.034 band
(mean last-ten reward 0.710--0.744) (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p5\_abstract.tex). The study proposes an
eight-item minimum reportable stack --- loss form; reference-policy and
KL handling; sampler, backend and precision including base-checkpoint
identity by revision or hash; per-step ZVF and gradient-utilisation
trajectory; group-size schedule; a disjoint held-out split;
decontamination plus a parser-robustness probe; and held-out pass@k
curves alongside pass@1 --- and formalises an RL-reproducibility threat
model with flip-risk grades R0--R5 (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p5\_abstract.tex). This is the standard
this thesis is written to.

P6, ``GRPO-Registry: A Machine-Readable Catalog of Group-Relative RL
Stacks and Their Variant Deltas'', asks whether stacks can be compared
mechanically rather than through prose (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p6\_abstract.tex). It defines
seven-field stack records corresponding to the MIN-REPORT-RL manifest
block, a per-component variant-delta status, three variant-delta records
verified against the DAPO, Dr.~GRPO and GSPO papers, twenty seed stack
entries, and a reference CLI with a 0--100 MIN-REPORT auditor badge
spanning 43--96 across the seed entries and a \texttt{stackdiff} command
that maps entries to an R0--R5 flip-risk verdict, flagging the
open-versus-closed ``DAPO'' pair as R5 (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p6\_abstract.tex). The study is
explicitly a resource paper rather than a second ranking, and it cites
the seventeen-fold exhibit only to motivate the schema (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p6\_abstract.tex).

P7, ``From Diagnostic to Controller: A Signal-Starvation Theory of GRPO
and an Adaptive Group-Size Intervention'', asks whether the diagnostic
of §4.3 can be turned into a control signal (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex). It models usable
learning signal per prompt as S = p(1 − p)(1 − h\_G(p)) with h\_G(p) =
p\^{}G + (1 − p)\^{}G, yielding three testable consequences: that the
expected ZVF indicator equals p\^{}G + (1 − p)\^{}G; that larger G
lowers ZVF; and that gradient magnitude tracks p(1 − p) (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex). The empirical checks
reproduce the predicted U-shape in the 368-run audit; a
model-by-group-size grid confirms the monotone G effect at interior
accuracy (Qwen3-32B: 0.33, 0.18 and 0.08 at G = 4, 8 and 16); and a
toy-scale open-backward-pass experiment finds corr(‖∇‖, p(1 − p)) =
+0.71 on a 0.5B model with synthetic arithmetic, reported as directional
only (source: platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex). The
study promotes pairwise-contrast density, PCD = (G − 1)/G · E{[}p(1 −
p){]}, as the control signal, and validates it by falsification: a
micro-jitter intervention collapses batch ZVF from 0.158 to 0.000 while
leaving PCD unchanged, showing that the two quantities are not measuring
the same thing (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex). Adaptive group size
matches the best fixed-recipe held-out gain (+0.575) at 186 rollouts,
while DAPO-style dynamic sampling drives ZVF to 0.00 at a 45\% rollout
cost increase; in the 2,560-observation controller trace, 1,723 of 1,867
escalation events (92.3\%) occur on all-correct groups and only 144 on
all-wrong groups (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex). The methodological
commitment that governs this study is stated at its head: adaptive group
size is not promoted, and no result establishes prospective superiority
over static G = 16, Dr.~GRPO or dynamic sampling (source:
platform\_hybrid/paper/sections/p7\_abstract.tex).

P8 occupies the eighth slot with a study outside the RL programme, ``LLM
vs.~XGBoost in Credit-Card Fraud: Sensor and Scribe, Not Scorer''
(source: platform\_hybrid/sem 4 work/README.md). Its question is whether
a large language model should be used as the fraud scorer or as a
component around one, and it is answered with tabular baselines reported
at AUC 0.7955, F1 0.356, precision 0.723 and recall 0.236 for the
boosted-tree model, against a Qwen3.5-4B scorer at accuracy 0.792 with
AUC 0.483, i.e.~chance-level ranking, at roughly eighty-five times lower
cost as a triage component (source: platform\_hybrid/sem 4
work/submissions/mtech-final-review/README.md;
platform\_hybrid/paper/archive/absorbed/P08\_fraud/sections/p8\_abstract.tex).
The roster evolved after the semester-4 title list was fixed, and the
present position is recorded honestly rather than smoothed over: the
fraud study is parked outside the ZVF thesis, contributes no RL
evidence, and is retained for its measurement discipline; the active P8
slot in the current paper roster is an exploratory workshop artifact
(source: platform\_hybrid/paper/PAPERS\_README.md). No result from P8 is
used anywhere in this thesis to support an RL claim.

\section{The Design of the E1--E14 Held-Out Evaluation
Campaign}\label{the-design-of-the-e1e14-held-out-evaluation-campaign}


\fig{fig_campaign_design}
{Design of the E1--E14 held-out evaluation campaign: one frozen actor, each suite graded by its own native evaluator at a pinned revision, with original-contract and replacement-scope results kept separate.}
{fig:campaign}



The evaluation campaign is the applied half of the programme and is
deliberately narrow in a way that makes its results interpretable: it
evaluates one actor against fourteen held-out benchmark suites under a
receipt, budget and decontamination regime, instead of training many
actors and comparing them. The single actor is the base model
\texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B} at commit \texttt{995ad96e…}, with adapter
\texttt{arvindcr4/pavlov-portfolio-qwen36-seed809-stepfinal-tinker-cf0ad8c1…}
at commit \texttt{64444133…}, in bfloat16 (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md). One actor fixes the
object of study: every reported per-suite figure describes the same
frozen policy, so a difference between two suites is a property of the
suite and not of a model swap. The design consequence is that the
campaign cannot support model-comparison claims at all, and it makes
none.

Evaluators are the suites' own scorers, used natively rather than
reimplemented, and they are pinned by revision. The surviving native
runners live under \texttt{zvf-program/flagship/}, including
\texttt{e1\_swe\_bench\_pro\_full\_eval.py},
\texttt{e11\_verilog\_eval\_decontamination.py},
\texttt{eval\_pavlov\_sdab.py}, \texttt{eval\_pavlov\_xlam.py},
\texttt{mle\_bench\_eval.py} and \texttt{eval\_apex\_agents.py} (source:
zvf-program/flagship/). The evaluation-side revisions pinned for the
decontamination audit are LAB-Bench
\texttt{998a8e0a40cf116c80e1b0e7a805ebb5fb9fa838}, AgentDojo
\texttt{089ed468cf3ed0322acc66b0211f26d9d90dbf60} and Omni-MATH
\texttt{40ba231d8f16e29ecd40e6407e2c8640145a8f62}, with the
training-side envelope pinned at API-Bank-RLVR
\texttt{bf67c42626f02c305514b1df16dcabc5fc616333} (597 rows, MIT) and
SWE-Gym \texttt{bb94ed9e39bbeb96a7fcbfb533b80f25a7fd59cb} (2,438 train
rows, MIT) (source:
outputs/public\_portfolio\_2026-09-05/decontamination/README.md). Using
the native scorer is a substantive choice and not a convenience: when
the Omni-MATH lane produced two rows whose judged outputs were truncated
before any equivalence verdict, the native scorer's omission of
missing-verdict rows was adjudicated as correct official behaviour
rather than a parser bug, and the lane closed with no re-judge because
re-judging would deviate from the official scorer and generate new
evidence with no effect on the score (source:
outputs/E1\_E14\_FINAL\_RESULTS\_2026-09-19.md).

Sampling is frozen rather than merely recorded: temperature 0.1, top-p
0.95, one sample per row, a maximum of 128 response tokens, with the
service seed fixed where the provider supports it and otherwise recorded
in the receipt (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md).
Prompt and response limits are 1,200 and 128 tokens respectively, and
the same source requires the strict verifier implementation to be hashed
before paid work, with a perfect call defined as a verifier score of
exactly 1.0 and no parser, target, split or verifier change permitted
mid-campaign (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md).
Freezing the sampler addresses the failure mode in which a benchmark
score quietly improves because decoding became more favourable between
runs; it also fixes the comparison between the base reference and the
adapter, which is evaluated side by side on untouched manifests (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md).

The receipt model is freeze-before-paid-work, and it is the part of the
campaign design that carries the most methodological weight. Before any
paid phase a run must record an immutable base-model revision, tokenizer
revision, container or environment digest and adapter revision; the
selection slice and the disjoint holdout are re-created from a pinned
dataset revision and sealed, are write-once, are not mounted in
training, and the word ``holdout'' is reserved for a suite or manifest
only once its split, task-ID, license, runtime and decontamination
receipts are complete (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md). The
selection slice is used for arm decisions and is therefore unsuitable
for confirmatory claims; the final evaluation manifests are never used
to choose an arm, alter a prompt, or re-run a failed suite with a more
favourable split (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md).
Tracking is verified before spend rather than after: an online Weights
\& Biases run must be initialised and its Tinker run identifier recorded
before the first paid step, every step records loss, reward,
configuration, seed, split identity and cumulative cost, and every
checkpoint is published to Hugging Face with a unique repository or
revision URL, with a failure of any of these stopping the run
fail-closed (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md). If a
receipt is missing, the phase is blocked rather than silently shortened
(source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md). At
handoff the protocol requires four separate evidence classes to be
reported apart: completed receipts; operational and provenance receipts;
prospective gates and budget projections; and admissible final results,
the last existing only if every gate passed (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md).

That last distinction has teeth in practice, and the campaign's
disposition record shows it. The portfolio-level protocol defines a
predeclared equal-domain macro average over sixteen domains, a
conjunctive coverage gate, an integrity gate, a global improvement gate
requiring the lower bound of a one-sided 95\% interval for the portfolio
difference to exceed zero, a domain no-regression guard with a
predeclared five-percentage-point tolerance, and a safety guard bounding
any violation increase by two percentage points --- and it states that a
cost cap may not be met by dropping domains or suites, and that a suite
or domain which is missing, underpowered, contaminated or outside the
cost cap must be labelled incomplete or blocked, ``not negative and not
positive'' (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md). The
portfolio training phase was never launched, so the campaign does not
report a portfolio result; the recorded outcome is a set of per-lane
evaluations of the one adapter, and the protocol's own instruction that
a partial subset is not a portfolio result and may not be relabelled as
one is honoured throughout (source:
zvf-program/flagship/PAVLOV\_EXPERIMENT\_PROTOCOL\_2026-08-09.md).

Lane dispositions are recorded in a seven-state taxonomy rather than as
pass or fail: COMPLETE, for lanes with verified full-coverage results
and no open gate (E8 public, E10 benign, E11); COMPLETE\_TERMINAL\_NOTE,
for a lane complete under the native protocol with a recorded terminal
note (E14 replacement); CLOSED\_EXTERNAL, for lanes with no local or
paid path, closed by written closure records (E3, E7, E8-private,
E10-private, E12, E14-private); PENDING\_QUOTA, for lanes gated on cloud
quota resolution alone (E6, E9); AMENDMENT\_ACCEPTED\_LAUNCH\_PENDING
(E2, E13); REBUILD\_READY\_LAUNCH\_PENDING (E1 wave10, E5 successor27);
and CLOSED\_PARTIAL (E4) (source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).
The taxonomy exists so that a lane which has not been measured is not
silently absorbed into a percentage. Carried alongside it is the
project's evidence rule, stated in the ledger of record and repeated in
every results artefact: original-contract and replacement-scope numbers
are never pooled (source:
outputs/PES\_Phase2\_Review\_2026-09-12/finish/Pending\_Experiments.md).
The rule is not cosmetic. The original-contract E1 lane and its
replacement scope are different suites with different task populations,
and a table that added them would produce a number that corresponds to
no benchmark.

Decontamination is handled as a declared deterministic procedure with
its own stated limits. The check hashes complete UTF-8 fields and then
normalised fields with SHA-256; for fields of at least eight tokens it
hashes each consecutive eight-token sequence and computes exact set
Jaccard similarity for every training and evaluation pair sharing a
shingle; and it tests whether an entire normalised evaluation field is a
token-bounded contiguous substring of a training field (source:
outputs/public\_portfolio\_2026-09-05/decontamination/README.md).
Against the pinned training envelope, the per-suite inventories were
1,967 LAB-Bench tasks over 13,113 text fields with 5,105 eligible for
eight-token near checks, 97 AgentDojo tasks over 71,966 fields with
9,382 eligible, and 4,428 Omni-MATH tasks over 13,284 fields with 9,394
eligible; the count of near pairs at or above 0.80 was zero in all three
suites, and the maximum eight-gram Jaccard similarity was 0 for
LAB-Bench and AgentDojo and 0.13414634146341464 for Omni-MATH, below the
declared 0.80 threshold, with all 1,338 candidate pairs sharing an
eight-gram examined (source:
outputs/public\_portfolio\_2026-09-05/decontamination/README.md). Short
exact matches of zero to three normalised tokens were retained and
enumerated rather than dropped --- the longest examples being seven
characters in the LAB and Omni inventories and at most seventeen raw
characters in AgentDojo, such as ``Paris'' and ``Conference Room A'' ---
and the audit states plainly that these collisions do not demonstrate
duplication of a task but do make an unqualified ``zero overlap'' claim
incorrect (source:
outputs/public\_portfolio\_2026-09-05/decontamination/README.md). The
audit's scope limits are recorded with equal care: it does not
reconstruct the exact 512 consumed training records, because the dirty
training transform and consumed task inventory were not recorded; it
establishes a content comparison against the verified historical source
envelope rather than proving global pretraining cleanliness, semantic
disjointness, character-edit disjointness or image cleanliness; ASCII
normalisation loses mathematical punctuation and non-ASCII text; and
fields shorter than eight tokens fall outside the near-match and
containment rule (source:
outputs/public\_portfolio\_2026-09-05/decontamination/README.md). The
audit itself is receipted: it re-verifies Weights \& Biases run
\texttt{bsv8vx04} against \texttt{training\_identity\_verified.json},
matches the 23,733 original source-field document hashes to the
